| R0720 | policies/remote-write-verification-policy.md | 17 | all | require | treat git log against the fetched remote as the authority on what landed, not the tool's response or the agent's recollection | a local clone exists | "Where a local clone exists, `git log` against the fetched remote is the authority on what landed — not the tool's response, and not the agent's recollection of what it sent." |

| R0728 | policies/remote-write-verification-policy.md | 35 | all | define | auth/permission errors, not-found errors, and any failure from the agent's own malformed call do not qualify | — | "**Not qualifying:** auth/permission errors; not-found errors (almost always a wrong path or ref); any failure from the agent's own malformed call — including a write that lands but commits wrong content (rule 3)." |

| R0827 | public-prose-criteria.md | 43 | writer | forbid | tier-blurring — an opinion dressed as grounded, a grounded claim dressed as proof | — | "Treat tier-blurring — an opinion dressed as grounded, a grounded claim dressed as proof — as a defect." |
| R0828 | public-prose-criteria.md | 44 | writer | require | carry a claim's tier by verb and frame, never by narrated self-correction | — | "Carry a claim's tier by verb and frame, never by narrated self-correction: keep \"what I noticed\"; cut \"I should be careful with my own story here.\"" |
| R0829 | public-prose-criteria.md | 50 | writer | require | define each governed or specialist term where it first appears for a public reader | — | "Define each governed or specialist term where it first appears for a public reader." |
| R0830 | public-prose-criteria.md | 51 | writer | require | flag the mismatch to the author, who decides which term the piece uses | an industry-standard term exists for a concept the piece names differently | "When an industry-standard term exists for a concept the piece names differently, flag the mismatch to the author; the author decides which the piece uses." |
| R0831 | public-prose-criteria.md | 57 | writer | require | anonymize by default in both directions: name criticized practices, not companies, products, or people, and be as sparing with names in praise | — | "Anonymize by default, in both directions: name criticized practices, not the companies, products, or people; be as sparing with names in praise." |
| R0832 | public-prose-criteria.md | 58 | writer | require | name a party only where there is a clear benefit in that instance, and state the benefit when proposing it | — | "Name a party only where there is a clear benefit in that instance, and state the benefit when proposing it." |
| R0833 | public-prose-criteria.md | 62 | writer | require | credit the known author of an idea, technique, phrase, or finding unless the work is trivial | — | "Attribute always. When the author of a piece of work — an idea, a technique, a phrase, a finding — is known, credit them, unless the work is trivial." |
| R0834 | public-prose-criteria.md | 64 | writer | forbid | taking credit by omission under cover of anonymize-by-default | — | "Anonymize-by-default governs targets and examples; it never licenses taking credit by omission." |
| R0835 | public-prose-criteria.md | 69 | writer | require | take length and structure from the author for the piece at hand, applying no standing shape | — | "Take length and structure from the author for the piece at hand; apply no standing shape." |
| R0836 | public-prose-criteria.md | 72 | writer | define | wordy means duplicated ideas, not duplicated words; a draft inside its word budget can still be wordy | — | "Wordy means duplicated ideas, not duplicated words; a draft inside its word budget can still be wordy." |
| R0837 | public-prose-criteria.md | 73 | writer | require | report an audit of the repeated ideas before cutting anything | the author suspects duplication | "When the author suspects duplication, report an audit of the repeated ideas before cutting anything." |
| R0838 | public-prose-criteria.md | 74 | writer | define | the author sets a word target and the writer chooses what goes | — | "The author sets a word target; the writer chooses what goes." |
| R0839 | public-prose-criteria.md | 75 | writer | require | target 10% under budget, not budget | after a wordy draft | "After a wordy draft, target 10% under budget, not budget." |
| R0840 | public-prose-criteria.md | 78 | writer | require | make a point once, flat | — | "Make a point once, flat." |
| R0841 | public-prose-criteria.md | 78 | writer | require | cut any contrast pair that restates a point from the other side | — | "Cut any contrast pair that restates it from the other side." |
| R0842 | public-prose-criteria.md | 79 | writer | require | cut any clause that explains the move it sits in | — | "Cut any clause that explains the move it sits in." |
| R0843 | public-prose-criteria.md | 80 | writer | require | cut scene-setting clauses, since stance carries on the verb | — | "Cut scene-setting clauses (\"when I sit down to build,\" \"from where I sit\"); stance carries on the verb." |
| R0844 | public-prose-criteria.md | 85 | writer | require | land every piece clean for a cold reader, though build across pieces is allowed | — | "Build across pieces is allowed; land every piece clean for a cold reader." |

| R0846 | public-prose-criteria.md | 91 | writer | require | prefer descriptive, searchable titles over clever ones, place key terms early, and keep the structure skimmable | — | "Prefer descriptive, searchable titles over clever ones; place key terms early; keep the structure skimmable." |
| R0847 | public-prose-criteria.md | 92 | writer | require | let readability win | discoverability and readability conflict | "Where discoverability and readability conflict, readability wins." |
| R0848 | public-prose-criteria.md | 93 | writer | forbid | altering the register for discoverability | — | "Never alter the register for discoverability." |
| R0849 | public-prose-criteria.md | 97 | writer | require | write venue-independent prose with no platform-specific formatting dependencies | — | "Write venue-independent prose with no platform-specific formatting dependencies." |
| R0850 | public-prose-criteria.md | 98 | writer | define | the canonical home and cross-post venues are the Voice document's | — | "The canonical home and cross-post venues are the Voice document's." |

Case-sensitive; no hyphen variants; no case folding; no other spelling." |
| R1340 | skills/directive-invariants.md | 223 | chief-of-staff | define | a line is eligible unless masked; fenced code blocks, blockquote lines, and HTML comments are masked, indented code blocks are not, and line endings are normalised before masking | — | "Eligible lines. A line is eligible unless it is masked. Fenced code blocks are masked: a backtick or tilde run of three or more of the same character, opened at column 0 or indented up to three spaces and closed by a run of at least the opening length; the fence lines themselves are masked, and an unclosed fence masks to end of file." |
| R1341 | skills/directive-invariants.md | 233 | chief-of-staff | define | stripping removes up to three leading spaces, then one list marker and its following space, then an ATX heading run and its space, then leading ** or __, applied once each in that order | — | "Stripping, applied to every eligible line before the literal is tested, removes up to three leading spaces; then one list marker (`-`, `*`, `+`, or digits followed by `.` or `)`) and the space after it; then an ATX heading run and its space; then leading `**` or `__`. Applied once each, in that order." |
| R1342 | skills/directive-invariants.md | 240 | chief-of-staff | define | the statement extent is the label line plus every following line up to the first blank line, over which form-membership is decided, with exactly one of the two forms required — zero fails, both fail | — | "Statement extent: the label line plus every following line up to the first blank line. Both, or neither." |

| R1347 | skills/directive-invariants.md | 261 | chief-of-staff | define | a marker is a line at column 0 that is either an ATX heading or an all-caps run of three or more characters from A-Z, 0-9, -, and single interior spaces, terminated by any character outside that set or by end of line; nothing else is a marker | — | "A marker is a line at column 0 that is either an ATX heading (one to six `#` characters, a space, then text; the token is the text after the run) or an all-caps run of three or more characters drawn from `A`-`Z`, `0`-`9`, `-`, and single interior spaces, terminated by any character outside that set or by end of line (the token is the run). Nothing else is a marker." |

| R1420 | skills/review-artifact.md | 85 | all | forbid | using the word agreed as a Verdict; ready means ready for Dave's agreement | — | "`Verdict` is deliberately **not** the word `agreed`. `ready` means ready for Dave's agreement." |

| R1422 | skills/review-artifact.md | 93 | all | require | write one entry per finding after the header, with finding id, severity, Claim, Location, Evidence, Consequence, Fix, and Related | — | "One entry per finding, after the header:" |

| R1424 | skills/review-artifact.md | 107 | all | require | distinguish in Evidence verified by running from inferred by reading | — | "`Evidence` distinguishing *verified by running* from *inferred by reading* is not optional." |

| R1426 | skills/review-artifact.md | 111 | all | define | an entry whose Consequence cannot state concretely what goes wrong is an observation | — | "`Consequence` is the field that does the work. If you cannot state concretely what goes wrong, the entry is an observation." |
| R1427 | skills/review-artifact.md | 114 | all | require | order blocking entries by weight | — | "Order `blocking` entries by weight — the schema has one bucket for a design hole and a wrong sentence, so the ordering is what carries the difference." |
| R1428 | skills/review-artifact.md | 119 | all | require | use prose only where judgment genuinely does not compress, never as the default and never replacing the verdict line | judgment genuinely does not compress | "Permitted where judgment genuinely does not compress — a material disagreement between reviewers, or a risk that needs an argument rather than an assertion. | R1446 | skills/spec-review-cycle.md | 65 | all | require | start a fresh conversation, opening with the reviewer findings and the reviewed commit SHA per document | — | "Start a fresh conversation. Open with the reviewer findings and the reviewed commit SHA per document." |

| R1448 | skills/spec-review-cycle.md | 69 | all | require | give a named defect class one ruling and dispose its instances under it | the reviewer names a class and lists its instances | "**A named defect class is triaged before its instances.** When the reviewer names a class and lists its instances, the class takes one ruling, and the instances dispose under it." |
| R1449 | skills/spec-review-cycle.md | 71 | all | forbid | triaging an instance on its own ahead of the class ruling | — | "No instance is triaged on its own ahead of the class ruling." |
